## Posted Column Convention

**All future updates MUST populate the `Posted` column with a precise date (YYYY-MM-DD), not vague indications like '1 week ago' / '2 weeks' / '1 day'.**

**Rules:**
- Format: `YYYY-MM-DD` (e.g. `2026-07-27`).
- Source: derive from LinkedIn's `Listed X days ago` text using the scan date as anchor (scan date minus X days).
- For LinkedIn tags like `Reposted`, `Actively reviewing`, or any deviation from 'Listed X days ago', still record the **computed posting date** as the Posted value and add a note in the Status column (e.g. `reposted 2026-07-24`).
- For jobs that show `Just now`, `Today`, `Yesterday`, or any non-numeric label that prevents computing a date, fall back to the scan date and add ` (LinkedIn gave no numeric ago)` to Status.
- If the Posted value was previously a vague ago-string, the next scan MUST overwrite it with a computed YYYY-MM-DD date (do not keep the old string).
- Daily scans: in the scan header summary, also report any job whose Posted value decreased (e.g. `2026-07-27 â†' 2026-07-22`) as that indicates a repost.

**Why this matters:** date-based diffing lets us reliably detect reposts, flag stale listings, and compute time-in-market. Vague strings (`1 week ago`) make all three impossible without manual inspection.

The next cron run MUST overwrite Posted for every row it touches. Migration for rows this script does not touch is documented in MEMORY.md.


**Row Order (mandatory after every daily update):**
- The main table MUST be re-sorted by `Posted` DESC (newest first). Ties broken by Role ASC for stability.
- The previous `#` (sequential ID) column is GONE â€" sorting was the only thing it made stable, and Posted-DESC sort is now both stable AND meaningful.
- Historical scan notes (`## Daily Scan: YYYY-MM-DD - ...`) keep their chronological order unchanged; the rule applies only to the live `### All AI Roles by Company` table.
